{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"[masked]","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t---\n2\tname: verify\n3\tdescription: Verify work against the project's own checks (type check, lint, tests, build, dependency audit) and prove each acceptance check by driving the running product, saving a per-check report to docs/evidence/stage-<n>/ for a plan stage. Use before calling any stage done, when asked to verify or test something, or when unsure a change works. Also when a message opens with \"JBIT verify\" (any case, after a space, comma or colon, as in \"JBit, verify\"), which works as `/jbit:verify`.\n4\t---\n5\t\n6\t# Verify\n7\t\n8\tRun it here or in a fresh `verifier` agent that built nothing. Start a role agent by its bare name (`verifier`) when the session's agent list has it, else as `jbit:verifier`, and never pass `model` or `effort` on the Agent call: its agent file sets those. Give it the stage number and this skill; tell it to read narrowly, stop when the brief is done, and hand back a report of 20 lines at most, then its full table under `## Report`. Never brief it to write `report.md`; Claude Code refuses that, so write it yourself. Started as the verifier, run it yourself.\n9\t\n10\t## Honest gaps\n11\t\n12\tA check that fails is reported as fail. A check you couldn't prove (app wouldn't start, no tool to drive it) is unproven, with the reason. Never round either up to pass, call a failure \"mostly passing\", or drop a row. A test that fails then passes on rerun is flaky: say so.\n13\t\n14\t## 1. The project's own checks\n15\t\n16\tFind how the project checks itself (`package.json` scripts, `Makefile`, CI config) and run whatever exists, in order: type check, lint, tests, build. All must pass, apart from `## Baseline` failures (report those as inherited). Any other failure belongs to this work unless it also fails on the last stage commit.\n17\t\n18\tNever weaken or skip a test or loosen a lint rule to get green; if a test is wrong, say why and fix it openly.\n19\t\n20\t## 2. Dependency audit\n21\t\n22\tRun the stack's audit tool if it's available without installing (`npm audit --omit=dev`, `pip-audit`, `cargo audit`, `govulncheck ./...`). Report high and critical findings in shipped dependencies with the fixed version; propose upgrades, don't make them.\n23\t\n24\t## 3. The acceptance checks, in the running product\n25\t\n26\tProve each check in `## Now` the way it names, against the real app:\n27\t\n28\t- **Web UI**: start the app (reuse a running dev server, or the built-in `run` skill), drive it with Playwright or the project's browser tooling, and screenshot and look at the states the check describes, the console for errors, and phone width when layout matters.\n29\t- **API or service**: real requests for success and at least one bad input.\n30\t- **CLI**: realistic arguments and a wrong one.\n31\t- **Library or logic**: the tests are the proof; confirm one covers the check and fails when the behaviour breaks (break it briefly if in doubt).\n32\t\n33\t**At the plan's last stage, re-run earlier stages' hand-proven checks** (proven by a run, request or screenshot, not a test), found in each `docs/evidence/stage-<k>/report.md`, the way they were proven then. One that passed then and fails now is a regression: report it as fail.\n34\t\n35\t## 4. Evidence\n36\t\n37\tFor a plan stage, save each check's proof in `docs/evidence/stage-<n>/` as you go (`<n>` is the stage in `## Now` unless asked about another), even when asked only for a report: raw output as one text file per proof named for what it shows (`tests.txt`, `cli-bad-arg.txt`) with the command on its first line, plus screenshots, traces and videos.\n38\t\n39\tIf `docs/evidence/.gitignore` is missing, create it:\n40\t\n41\t```\n42\t# Screenshots, traces and videos stay local.
